=== SETUP INITIALIZATION ===
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Odysseus — first-time setup script.
Creates data directories, initializes the database, and sets up an
initial admin user. Safe to re-run (skips what already exists).
"""
import os
import platform
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
BASE_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, BASE_DIR)
from src.constants import (
DATA_DIR, AUTH_FILE, UPLOAD_DIR, PERSONAL_DIR,
PERSONAL_UPLOADS_DIR,
TTS_CACHE_DIR, GENERATED_IMAGES_DIR,
DEEP_RESEARCH_DIR, CHROMA_DIR,
RAG_DIR, MEMORY_VECTORS_DIR, AGENT_WORKSPACE_DIR,
PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH,
)
from core.auth import RESERVED_USERNAMES
DIRS = [
DATA_DIR,
UPLOAD_DIR,
PERSONAL_DIR,
PERSONAL_UPLOADS_DIR,
TTS_CACHE_DIR,
GENERATED_IMAGES_DIR,
DEEP_RESEARCH_DIR,
CHROMA_DIR,
RAG_DIR,
MEMORY_VECTORS_DIR,
AGENT_WORKSPACE_DIR,
os.path.join(BASE_DIR, "logs"),
]
def create_dirs():
for d in DIRS:
os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
print(f" [ok] {os.path.relpath(d, BASE_DIR)}/")
def init_database():
"""Create all SQLAlchemy tables."""
sys.path.insert(0, BASE_DIR)
os.environ.setdefault("DATABASE_URL",
f"sqlite:///{os.path.join(DATA_DIR, 'app.db')}")
from core.database import Base, engine
Base.metadata.create_all(bind=engine)
print(" [ok] Database initialized")
def _prompt_admin_credentials():
"""Interactively ask for admin username and password when
running in a terminal."""
import getpass
print()
print(" Set up your admin account:")
print(" (Press Enter to accept defaults)")
print()
while True:
username = input(" Username [admin]: ").strip().lower()
if not username:
username = "admin"
if username in RESERVED_USERNAMES:
print(f" '{username}' is a reserved username. Choose
another.")
continue
break
while True:
password = getpass.getpass(" Password: ")
if not password:
print(" Password cannot be empty.")
continue
if len(password) < PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH:
print(f" Password must be at least
{PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH} characters.")
continue
confirm = getpass.getpass(" Confirm password: ")
if password != confirm:
print(" Passwords don't match. Try again.")
continue
break
return username, password
def create_default_admin():
"""Create an initial admin user if none exists."""
auth_path = AUTH_FILE
if os.path.exists(auth_path):
print(" [skip] auth.json already exists")
return "exists"
try:
import bcrypt
import json
# Priority: env vars > interactive prompt > random password
username = os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_USER",
"").strip().lower()
password = os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD",
"").strip()
if username and password:
# Both provided via env — validate before using
if username in RESERVED_USERNAMES:
print(f" [error] ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_USER '{username}' is a
reserved username")
return "failed"
if len(password) < PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH:
print(f" [error] ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD must be
at least {PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH} characters")
return "failed"
elif sys.stdin.isatty() and not
os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_SKIP_ADMIN_PROMPT"):
# Interactive terminal — ask the user
username, password = _prompt_admin_credentials()
else:
# Non-interactive (Docker, CI) — fall back to generated
password
username = username or "admin"
password = password or
__import__("secrets").token_urlsafe(18)
username = username or "admin"
hashed = bcrypt.hashpw(password.encode(),
bcrypt.gensalt()).decode()
auth_data = {
"users": {
username: {
"password_hash": hashed,
"is_admin": True,
}
}
}
with open(auth_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
json.dump(auth_data, f, indent=2)
if sys.stdin.isatty() and not
os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD"):
print(f" [ok] Admin account created ({username})")
else:
print(f" [ok] Initial admin user created ({username})")
if not os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD"):
print(f" Temporary password: {password}")
print(f" ** Change it after first login. Set
ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD to choose your own. **")
return "created"
except ImportError as e:
if "incompatible architecture" in str(e).lower():
# bcrypt is present but built for the wrong CPU architecture
— the
# same Apple Silicon mismatch check_arch() guards against,
caught here
# for the rarer case of an x86 wheel inside an arm64 venv.
print(" [error] bcrypt loaded with the wrong CPU
architecture.")
print(" Rebuild the venv with an arm64 Python:")
print(" rm -rf venv &&
/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.11 -m venv venv")
print(" ./venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt")
return "skipped"
print(" [warn] bcrypt not installed — skipping admin user
creation")
print(" Run: pip install bcrypt")
return "skipped"
print("\n Rebuild the environment with Homebrew's arm64
Python:")
print(" brew install python@3.11 # if you don't have
it yet")
print(" rm -rf venv")
print(" /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.11 -m venv venv")
print(" ./venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt")
print(" ./venv/bin/python setup.py")
print("\n Tip: ./start-macos.sh does all of this with the right
Python.\n")
sys.exit(1)
def main():
print("\n=== Odysseus Setup ===\n")
# Load .env so pre-seeded ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_USER /
ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD (and
# other deployment vars) are honored on native installs, not just
when they
# are exported in the shell. Mirrors app.py: encoding="utf-8-sig"
tolerates a
# UTF-8 BOM in a Notepad-saved .env. load_dotenv does not
override already
# exported OS env vars, so the existing precedence is preserved.
python-dotenv
# is a hard dependency (requirements.txt) and is verified by
check_deps below.
from dotenv import load_dotenv
load_dotenv(os.path.join(BASE_DIR, ".env"), encoding="utf-8-
sig")
# Fail fast with a clear message if the CPU architecture is wrong
(Apple
# Silicon under an x86/Rosetta Python) before importing
anything native.
check_arch()
print("1. Creating directories...")
create_dirs()
print("\n2. Environment file...")
create_env()
print("\n3. Checking dependencies...")
check_deps()
print("\n4. Initializing database...")
try:
init_database()
except Exception as e:
print(f" [warn] Database init failed: {e}")
print(" This is OK if dependencies aren't installed yet.")
print("\n5. Creating initial admin...")
admin_status = "failed"
try:
admin_status = create_default_admin()
except Exception as e:
print(f" [warn] Admin creation failed: {e}")
admin_status = "failed"
print("\n=== Setup complete ===")
# start-macos.sh launches the server itself (on its own port) right
after
# this, so suppress the manual hint there to avoid a contradictory
URL.
if not os.getenv("ODYSSEUS_SKIP_RUN_HINT"):
print(f"\nStart the server with:")
print(f" python -m uvicorn app:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port
7000")
print(f"\nThen open http://localhost:7000")
# Cleaned, action-focused final instruction strings
if admin_status == "created":
print("Login with your admin credentials.\n")
elif admin_status == "exists":
print("Login with your existing admin credentials.\n")
elif admin_status == "skipped":
print("Admin creation did not happen: dependencies are
missing.\nRun 'pip install bcrypt' and rerun setup.\n")
elif admin_status == "failed":
print("Admin creation did not happen: a system or file error
occurred.\nCheck write permissions for the 'data' directory and
rerun setup.\n")
else: # handling "failed" or any unhandled edge case
print("Admin creation did not happen: a system or file error
occurred.\nCheck write permissions for the 'data' directory and
rerun setup.\n")

=== SECURITY AND ADMIN SETTINGS ===
#
===========================================
=================
# Auth & Security
#
===========================================
=================
# Optional backend workspace used automatically by the WebUI
when no workspace
# is saved in the browser. This must be a directory visible to the
backend;
# with host-workspace mapping, a host path is translated before
vetting.
# ODYSSEUS_WORKSPACE_DEFAULT=/workspace/project
# Enable authentication (default: true)
# AUTH_ENABLED=true
# Host bind address and port for the Odysseus web UI in Docker
Compose.
# Keep APP_BIND on loopback unless you intentionally want
LAN/reverse-proxy access.
# APP_BIND=127.0.0.1
# Change this if another local service already uses 7000 (macOS
AirPlay often does).
# APP_PORT=7011
# Optional HTTP address advertised in companion/mobile pairing
codes. Set this
# when Docker would otherwise advertise a container address or
loopback. Use a
# LAN or Tailscale IPv4 address, a single-label hostname, or an
mDNS *.local
# name that the phone can reach. HTTPS and public hostnames are
not supported
# by the current companion client. Do not include credentials, a
path, query,
# or fragment.
# COMPANION_BASE_URL=http://192.168.1.50:7000
# Development-only auth bypass for loopback requests.
# Keep false for Docker, LAN, reverse proxy, and any shared
deployment.
# LOCALHOST_BYPASS=false
# Skip the external-context exact-approval pause for unattended
local agents.
# Keep false for shared or internet-exposed deployments.
# Post-external-context tool approval gate. On by default; set to 0
only for
# trusted single-user setups that accept injected content reaching
side-effect tools.
# ODYSSEUS_TOOL_APPROVAL_GATE=1
# Mark session cookies Secure. Left unset, this follows the request
scheme:
# an HTTPS login gets a Secure cookie, a plain-HTTP one does not.
Set true to
# force it on, or false to force it off while you still serve plain HTTP.
# Upgrading: this used to default to false. Drop a leftover
SECURE_COOKIES=false
# from your .env unless you still need that escape hatch — it keeps
HTTPS logins
# on a non-Secure cookie.
# SECURE_COOKIES=true
# Optional: pre-seed the first admin password during setup.
# Do not commit a real password.
# ODYSSEUS_ADMIN_PASSWORD=change_me_before_first_boot
# CORS allowed origins (default: localhost-only; restrict to your
public origin in production)
# ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:7000,http://localhost:8000
#
===========================================
=================
# ChromaDB (vector store)
#
===========================================
=================
# ChromaDB service host.
# Manual host run: localhost:8100 when using docker run -p
8100:8000 chromadb/chroma.
# Docker Compose overrides these to chromadb:8000 for innetwork access.
# CHROMADB_HOST=localhost
# CHROMADB_PORT=8100
# Docker Compose host-port bind addresses for bundled services.
# Defaults are loopback-only for safety. To expose ntfy only on
Tailscale,
# set NTFY_BIND to your host's Tailscale IP and update
NTFY_BASE_URL.
# CHROMADB_BIND=127.0.0.1
# NTFY_BIND=127.0.0.1
# NTFY_BASE_URL=http://localhost:8091
# Example:
# NTFY_BIND=100.x.y.z

Manual startup policy fixed
ChromaDB now has restart policy no . It will not automatically restart after a host reboot.
Firewall decision: the existing broad port allowance already permits TCP port 7011 from the
whole network. Rather than remove that rule and risk breaking other services, we'll add a
higher-priority exception: reject access to port 7011 from every IPv4 address except your PC
( 192.168.1.169 ).

1. LLM — not configured yet
Odysseus does not include a ready-to-use LLM simply because the WebUI is running. Its AI
features need a model provider configured.
We should decide between:

Local LLM: a model served locally, independently of your existing LocalAI project.
Both: local and cloud models, selectable in Odysseus.
We have not installed a separate local model server or downloaded a model for Odysseus.
2. Local Agent — to configure
The application starts, but that does not mean its Agent is ready to work. We need to:
Select its LLM.
Inspect its available tools and permissions.
Configure approval requirements.
Test it with harmless tasks before granting any meaningful capabilities.
3. Email — disable it
The startup log shows that Odysseus loaded its built-in Email MCP server and registered 19
tools. That does not prove it has access to a mailbox, but we should not leave this capability
enabled.
Your requirement is clear: no email monitoring, reading, or mailbox access. We should disable
the Email integration and verify that it is no longer registered. No mail credentials should be
configured.
4. Cloud AI — to configure
We should add the cloud providers you choose, without committing to paid API usage.
Configure provider credentials locally in Odysseus, never in chat.
My recommendation: start with one cloud provider, verify chat and Agent operation, then add
alternatives. Keep provider selection manual.

"""
builtin_mcp.py
Auto-registration of built-in MCP servers on startup.
Each server runs as a stdio subprocess managed by McpManager.
"""
import asyncio
import json
import logging
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
from core.platform_compat import IS_WINDOWS, which_tool
from src.runtime_paths import get_app_root
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
def _find_npx() -> str:
"""Find the npx binary, checking common locations if not on
PATH.
On Windows the shim is npx.cmd, which which_tool resolves
via PATHEXT.
"""
npx = which_tool("npx")
if npx:
return npx
if IS_WINDOWS:
# Minimal-PATH fallbacks: npm's global bin lives under
%APPDATA%\npm,
# and node's installer dir carries npx.cmd alongside node.exe.
appdata = os.environ.get("APPDATA", os.path.expanduser("~"))
for candidate in (
os.path.join(appdata, "npm", "npx.cmd"),
r"C:\Program Files\nodejs\npx.cmd",
):
if os.path.isfile(candidate):
return candidate
node = which_tool("node")
if node:
cand = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(node), "npx.cmd")
if os.path.isfile(cand):
return cand
return "npx.cmd" # fallback, will fail with a clear error
# Common POSIX locations when PATH is minimal (e.g. systemd)
for candidate in [
os.path.expanduser("~/.npm-global/bin/npx"),
os.path.expanduser("~/.local/bin/npx"),
"/usr/local/bin/npx",
"/usr/bin/npx",
]:
if os.path.isfile(candidate):
return candidate
# Try to find node and use npx from same dir
node = shutil.which("node")
if node:
npx_candidate = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(node), "npx")
if os.path.isfile(npx_candidate):
return npx_candidate
return "npx" # fallback, will fail with a clear error
# Server definitions: id -> (script path relative to project root,
display name)
#
# bash / python / filesystem / web_search were folded into native
in-process
# execution (src/tool_execution.py:_direct_fallback). Those trivial
subprocess
# wrappers are gone.
#
# image_gen / memory / rag / email still run as stdio MCP servers
— each
# carries hundreds of LOC of unique IMAP / HTTP / manager logic
not worth
# duplicating into the native path right now.
_BUILTIN_SERVERS = {
"image_gen": ("mcp_servers/image_gen_server.py", "Built-in:
Image Generation"),
"memory": ("mcp_servers/memory_server.py", "Built-in:
Memory"),
"rag": ("mcp_servers/rag_server.py", "Built-in: RAG"),
"email": ("mcp_servers/email_server.py", "Built-in: Email"),
}
# NPX-based built-in servers (run via npx, not Python)
_BUILTIN_NPX_SERVERS = {
"builtin_browser": {
"name": "Built-in: Browser",
"command": "npx",
"args": ["-y", "@playwright/mcp@latest", "--headless", "--caps",
"vision"],
}
}
# Global flag to disable MCP if there are compatibility issues
MCP_DISABLED = os.environ.get("ODYSSEUS_DISABLE_MCP",
"").lower() in ("1", "true", "yes")
BROWSER_MCP_REQUIRE_CACHE =
os.environ.get("ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_MCP_REQUIRE_CACHE",
"").lower() in ("1", "true", "yes")
# Strong references to the fire-and-forget startup tasks scheduled
below.
# asyncio only keeps weak references to tasks created via
create_task, so
# without this the GC can collect a task mid-execution and the
server
# registration silently never runs. Mirrors _spawn_bg in
routes/chat_helpers.py.
_BG_TASKS: set[asyncio.Task] = set()
def _spawn_bg(coro) -> asyncio.Task:
"""Schedule a background task and hold a strong reference until
it finishes."""
task = asyncio.create_task(coro)
_BG_TASKS.add(task)
task.add_done_callback(_BG_TASKS.discard)
return task
def _find_browser_executable() -> str:
"""Find a browser binary for the built-in Playwright MCP server.
Docker images ship Debian's chromium; desktop installs may
already have
Chrome/Chromium in a conventional location. If nothing is
found, return an
empty string and let Playwright MCP use its own default
browser/channel.
"""
configured =
os.environ.get("ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE", "").strip()
if configured:
return configured
for name in ("google-chrome", "chromium", "chromiumbrowser"):
path = shutil.which(name)
if path:
return path
for candidate in (
"/opt/google/chrome/chrome",
"/usr/bin/google-chrome",
"/usr/bin/chromium",
"/usr/bin/chromium-browser",
):
if os.path.isfile(candidate):
return candidate
return ""
def _browser_mcp_args(args: list[str]) -> list[str]:
"""Return Playwright MCP args with a concrete browser
executable when found."""
out = list(args or [])
if "--executable-path" not in out:
browser = _find_browser_executable()
if browser:
out.extend(["--executable-path", browser])
if os.environ.get("ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_ISOLATED", "1").lower()
not in ("0", "false", "no"):
if "--isolated" not in out and "--user-data-dir" not in out:
out.append("--isolated")
if os.environ.get("ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_NO_SANDBOX",
"1").lower() not in ("0", "false", "no"):
if "--no-sandbox" not in out and "--sandbox" not in out:
out.append("--no-sandbox")
return out
def builtin_python_env(base_dir: str) -> dict[str, str]:
"""Environment for built-in Python MCP subprocesses.
The app root must be importable so mcp_servers can import
local modules, but
replacing PYTHONPATH entirely hides site-packages in
container/dev launches
that rely on PYTHONPATH for their active environment.
"""
existing = os.environ.get("PYTHONPATH", "")
parts = [base_dir]
for item in existing.split(os.pathsep):
if item and item not in parts:
parts.append(item)
return {"PYTHONPATH": os.pathsep.join(parts)}

command=python,
args=[script_path],
env=builtin_python_env(base_dir),

async def register_builtin_servers(mcp_manager):
"""Connect all built-in MCP servers to the manager."""
if MCP_DISABLED:
logger.info("Built-in MCP servers disabled via
ODYSSEUS_DISABLE_MCP")
return
base_dir = get_app_root()
python = sys.executable
async def _connect_python_server(server_id: str, script_path: str,
name: str):

server_id=server_id,
name=name,
transport="stdio",
command=python,
args=[script_path],
env=builtin_python_env(base_dir),
)
if ok:
logger.info(f"Built-in MCP server registered: {name}")
await mcp_manager.hold_owned_connection(server_id)
else:
logger.warning(f"Built-in MCP server failed to connect:
{name}")
except asyncio.CancelledError:
logger.warning(f"Built-in MCP server {name} cancelled")
raise
except BaseException as e:
logger.warning(f"Built-in MCP server {name} error:
{type(e).__name__}: {e}")
for server_id, (script, name) in _BUILTIN_SERVERS.items():
script_path = os.path.join(base_dir, script)
if not os.path.exists(script_path):
logger.warning(f"Built-in MCP server script not found:
{script_path}")
continue
_spawn_bg(_connect_python_server(server_id, script_path,
name))
# Register NPX-based servers in the background (they take
longer to start)
npx_path = _find_npx()
logger.info(f"NPX binary resolved to: {npx_path}")
async def _start_npx_servers():
await asyncio.sleep(3) # let Python servers finish first
for server_id, cfg in _BUILTIN_NPX_SERVERS.items():
# Browser automation is a shipped built-in, so the default
path
# lets npx -y install @playwright/mcp on first start. Lockeddown
# installs can opt back into the old no-network startup
behavior
# with ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_MCP_REQUIRE_CACHE=1.
args = _browser_mcp_args(cfg["args"]) if server_id ==
"builtin_browser" else list(cfg["args"])
pkg_spec = _npx_package_from_args(args)
if BROWSER_MCP_REQUIRE_CACHE and pkg_spec and not
await _is_npx_package_cached(npx_path, pkg_spec):
logger.warning(
f"{cfg['name']} is not available.\n"
f" Reason: npm package {pkg_spec!r} is not installed in
the npx cache.\n"
f" Impact: tools provided by this MCP server will be
unavailable.\n"
f" Fix: {os.path.basename(npx_path)} -y {pkg_spec} --
version\n"
f" (run once, then restart Odysseus)\n"
f" Notes:
ODYSSEUS_BROWSER_MCP_REQUIRE_CACHE=1 is set, "
f"so Odysseus will not install browser automation on
startup."
)
continue
logger.info(f"Starting NPX server: {cfg['name']} ({npx_path} {'
'.join(args)})")
=== EMAIL MCP REFERENCES IN MANAGER ===
output_parts = []
images = []
for content in result.content:
if hasattr(content, 'text'):
output_parts.append(content.text)
elif getattr(content, 'type', '') == 'image' and hasattr(content,
'data'):
# Image content (e.g. Playwright screenshots)
mime = getattr(content, 'mimeType', 'image/png')
images.append({"data": content.data, "mimeType": mime})
output_parts.append(f"[Screenshot captured ({mime})]")
elif hasattr(content, 'data'):
output_parts.append(str(content.data))
output = "\n".join(output_parts)
is_error = getattr(result, 'isError', False)
result_dict = {
"stdout": output if not is_error else "",
"stderr": output if is_error else "",
"exit_code": 1 if is_error else 0,
}
if is_error and output:
result_dict["untrusted_content"] = True
if images:
result_dict["images"] = images
return result_dict
async def _reconnect_builtin(self, server_id: str) -> bool:
"""Tear down and reconnect a crashed builtin MCP server."""
import sys
from src.builtin_mcp import _BUILTIN_SERVERS,
builtin_python_env
if server_id not in _BUILTIN_SERVERS:
return False
script_rel, name = _BUILTIN_SERVERS[server_id]
base_dir = get_app_root()
script_path = os.path.join(base_dir, script_rel)
# Clean up old connection
await self.disconnect_server(server_id)
try:
ok = await self.connect_server(
server_id=server_id,
name=name,
transport="stdio",
command=sys.executable,
args=[script_path],
env=builtin_python_env(base_dir),
)
if ok:
logger.info(f"Reconnected builtin MCP server: {name}")
return ok
except Exception as e:
logger.error(f"Failed to reconnect builtin MCP server {name}:
{e}")
return False

My assessment
Best initial providers
1 Groq — first integration; fast and attractive privacy terms.
2 Cloudflare — particularly interesting privacy commitments and daily free allocation.
3
OpenAI — valuable model quality, but not a free API option under your current
ChatGPT Free plan.

Mistral and Cerebras are good additional independent providers. OpenRouter comes last
because it adds another intermediary and makes privacy depend on the underlying model
provider.
How we should manage the free allowances
I recommend manual provider selection, exactly as you previously wanted.
Keep a provider selected for the duration of a conversation.
Switch manually when its allowance is exhausted.
Track each provider's remaining quota independently.
No automatic fallback that might unexpectedly send a conversation to another company.
No paid API usage or automatic credit purchases.
This also gives us a way to compare model quality rather than assuming that every model is
equally capable.
Security: essential before giving the Agent access
For the Steam Deck supervisor project, I would establish these boundaries before enabling
powerful Agent tools:
Resource Cloud access
Project documentation Allowed, selectively
Storage inventory Allowed, sanitized
Service status and logs Allowed, sanitized
SSH private keys Forbidden
I strongly favour an allowlist of approved directories and tools, rather than giving the Agent
broad filesystem access and trying to exclude sensitive folders afterwards.
Also, credentials must never be included in prompts, logs, RAG ingestion or memory. The
Agent should not be allowed to read arbitrary environment variables or inspect browser
profiles.
One important point about training and knowledge
We should distinguish three things:
1. Model training: changing the actual model weights. We are not doing that.
2. Persistent memory: storing useful facts and decisions locally in Odysseus.
3. Skills: reusable procedures the Agent can create and improve.
Our objective is the second and third. Provider switching should not erase local memory or
skills. We will verify that Odysseus actually shares them across providers and both Chat and
Agent modes before relying on that promise.

settings and confirm whether its WebUI supports multiple independent providers and manual
model switching without changing the Agent's memory. That is the foundation for the whole

3. Mandatory WebUI and logging features
Here is what I propose we establish as acceptance criteria.

Requirement Expected behaviour
Provider connection Visible status: Online / Offline / Unknown
API authentication Clearly distinguish invalid key from connection failure
Rate limits Show quota or rate-limit errors
API errors Understandable message in WebUI
Detailed diagnostics Full sanitized technical details in logs
Request tracking Timestamp, provider, model, request ID
Performance Response time and token usage where available
I would also like a Test Connection action for each provider.
Suggested status states:
ONLINE API successfully tested
DEGRADED Rate limits or intermittent failures
OFFLINE Connection unavailable
AUTH ERROR Invalid or rejected credentials
NOT TESTED Not yet verified
4. Odysseus WebUI customization
Yes, customization is something we should investigate.
I propose three levels:
1. Settings configuration: use existing options wherever possible.
2. Custom dashboard/menu: add provider status, diagnostics and useful shortcuts.
3. Custom HTML/CSS/JS: only if necessary, preferably through isolated files rather than
modifying core application logic.
We must first inspect how the WebUI is structured. I will not promise that arbitrary HTML
changes are safe until we understand its frontend architecture.
Any customization should be reversible, backed up and tested after application updates.

For every API request, I want enough information to reconstruct a failure without exposing
credentials:
Provider selection Clearly identify the active provider and model
Debugging Separate logs for each provider
Secret protection Never expose API keys in logs or UI

I inspected the official repository, including its frontend organization, application entry point,

My recommendation is clear:

There is an important distinction between preparing the architecture for seven providers and
activating seven providers simultaneously.

2. Should we integrate all seven providers at once?
No. But we should prepare the common architecture for all seven at once.
This is the important distinction:

One shared architecture, seven independent provider configurations.
We should not build seven different API implementations if a shared provider interface can
handle them.
Each provider will have:
Its own API key.
Its own configuration.
Its own logs.
Its own connection test.
Its own health status.
But the common infrastructure handles authentication, request tracking, error classification and
diagnostics.
3. Recommended implementation sequence
Build the common foundation
Create provider directory structure.
Establish host-to-container API key access.
Define configuration and logging standards.
Define provider health/status API.
Keep secrets out of logs.
Integrate Groq only
Test valid key, invalid key, unavailable endpoint, unsupported model, rate limit and
successful response.
Build the custom provider dashboard
Now that we have a working provider, we can build the UI against real connection and
diagnostic data rather than hypothetical data.
Integrate Cloudflare
Reuse the same architecture and test it independently.

4. Important distinction about provider connections
We can configure all seven providers simultaneously, each with its own credentials. That does
not mean we should send requests to all seven simultaneously.
Your intended arrangement is:
Seven providers registered.
Seven independent health states.
One manually selected provider for each conversation.
No automatic fallback.
No automatic paid usage.
This is entirely consistent with the architecture we are designing.
5. Final decision
Recommended plan
Add the remaining providers one by one
OpenAI → Gemini → Mistral → Cerebras → OpenRouter.
Perform a complete integration test
Check provider switching, connection status, error handling, logging, secret protection and
persistence across restarts.
Work item Decision
Provider directory structure Build once
Key management Build once
Logging and diagnostics Build once
Provider status API Build once
Groq integration First
Custom provider dashboard After Groq validation
Remaining six integrations One at a time
Core HTML rewrite Avoid
Git branch Stay on main

import logging.handlers
from core.constants import DATA_DIR
_root_logger = logging.getLogger()
_root_logger.setLevel(logging.INFO)
_formatter = logging.Formatter('%(asctime)s - %(name)s - %
(levelname)s - %(message)s')
# Clear existing handlers to avoid duplicates
for _h in list(_root_logger.handlers):
_root_logger.removeHandler(_h)
_console_h = logging.StreamHandler()
_console_h.setFormatter(_formatter)
_root_logger.addHandler(_console_h)
try:
_log_dir = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "logs")
os.makedirs(_log_dir, exist_ok=True)
_log_file = os.path.join(_log_dir, "app.log")
# RotatingFileHandler is not multi-process safe (e.g. if uvicorn is
run with --workers N).
# Odysseus is single-process by convention, so this is acceptable,
but be aware that
# concurrent log rotation issues can arise if multiple workers are
configured.
_file_h = logging.handlers.RotatingFileHandler(
_log_file, maxBytes=5 * 1024 * 1024, backupCount=3,
encoding="utf-8"
)
_file_h.setFormatter(_formatter)
_root_logger.addHandler(_file_h)
except Exception as e:
_root_logger.warning(f"Failed to initialize file logging handler
(falling back to console-only): {e}")

cat > decktools/odysseus_start.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -Eeuo pipefail
PROJECT_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject"
TOOLS_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/decktools"
RUNTIME_DIR="$TOOLS_DIR/runtime"
PID_FILE="$RUNTIME_DIR/odysseus.pid"
CHROMA_MARKER="$RUNTIME_DIR/chromadb.started-by-odysseus"
LOG_FILE="$PROJECT_DIR/data/logs/uvicorn-console.log"
CHROMA_NAME="odysseus-chromadb"
CHROMA_HEALTH_URL="http://127.0.0.1:8100/api/v2/heartbeat"
APP_HEALTH_URL="http://127.0.0.1:7011/"
cd "$PROJECT_DIR"
mkdir -p "$RUNTIME_DIR" "$PROJECT_DIR/data/logs"
host_podman() {
distrobox-host-exec podman "$@"
}
echo "===== ODYSSEUS START ====="
# Prevent a duplicate launch if the recorded process is still alive.
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]]; then
OLD_PID="$(cat "$PID_FILE")"
if [[ "$OLD_PID" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] && kill -0 "$OLD_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
if tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$OLD_PID/cmdline" 2>/dev/null |
grep -Fq '.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app'; then
echo "Odysseus is already running (PID $OLD_PID)."
exit 0
fi
fi
echo "Removing stale or invalid PID file."
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
# Also detect an untracked instance before starting anything.
if pgrep -af '.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app' >/dev/null; then
echo "An existing Uvicorn instance was detected."
echo "Refusing to start a duplicate. Inspect processes first."
exit 1
fi
# Check ChromaDB before deciding whether to start it.
if host_podman container exists "$CHROMA_NAME"; then
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect "$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" != "true" ]]; then
echo "Starting existing ChromaDB container..."
host_podman start "$CHROMA_NAME" >/dev/null
touch "$CHROMA_MARKER"
else
echo "ChromaDB is already running; ownership remains unchanged."
fi
else
echo "ERROR: Dedicated ChromaDB container was not found."
exit 1
fi
# Wait up to 30 seconds for ChromaDB.
echo "Waiting for ChromaDB health..."
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
if curl -fsS --max-time 2 "$CHROMA_HEALTH_URL" >/dev/null; then
echo "ChromaDB is healthy."
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if ! curl -fsS --max-time 3 "$CHROMA_HEALTH_URL" >/dev/null; then
echo "ERROR: ChromaDB did not become healthy."
exit 1
fi
# Launch the application detached from the terminal.
echo "Starting Odysseus in the background..."
nohup setsid "$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python" -m uvicorn app:app \
--host 0.0.0.0 --port 7011 \
>> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 </dev/null &
APP_PID=$!

process tracking and failure cleanup. The script is not yet approved for operational use.
Paste the output of the creation and syntax checks.

# Wait up to 30 seconds for the HTTP endpoint.
echo "Waiting for Odysseus HTTP response..."
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
if ! kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus process exited during startup."
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
exit 1
fi
HTTP_CODE="$(curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \
--max-time 2 "$APP_HEALTH_URL" 2>/dev/null || true)"
if [[ "$HTTP_CODE" =~ ^[23][0-9][0-9]$ ]]; then
echo "Odysseus is responding (HTTP $HTTP_CODE)."
echo "PID: $APP_PID"
echo "Console log: $LOG_FILE"
exit 0
fi
sleep 1
done
echo "ERROR: Odysseus did not respond within 30 seconds."
echo "Inspect: $LOG_FILE"
exit 1
EOF

START script — review
The issue: if Odysseus fails to respond within 30 seconds, the script exits but does not attempt
to terminate the process it just launched. That could leave an orphaned application running.

Step 2.2.1 — Correct startup failure handling
I propose adding a controlled cleanup function that:
1. Verifies the PID still belongs to the Odysseus Uvicorn process.
2. Sends SIGTERM on startup failure.
3. Waits briefly for graceful shutdown.
4. Removes the PID file only after confirming the process has stopped.
5. Leaves ChromaDB running, with its ownership marker intact for the eventual STOP script.
Do not execute the START script yet.
Next, I'll provide a small, targeted edit and a verification command. We will approve the
corrected START script before its first launch.

You said:
ChatGPT said:
ok continue
Good. We'll make a targeted correction, not rewrite the script.
I also want to remove setsid from the launch command. It can introduce a separate session
process, making $! less reliable as the actual Uvicorn PID. nohup with redirected input/output
is sufficient for this background launch and makes PID tracking simpler.
The revised startup-failure handling will verify the process identity before sending SIGTERM . It
will not blindly terminate a process just because a PID file exists.
Step 2.2.1 — Correct START script
Execution environment: ODYSSEUS — odysseus-box (CONTAINER)
This replaces only the section beginning at # Launch the application detached from the
terminal . It leaves the rest of the script untouched.
Run this edit:
cd /home/deck/OdysseusProject || exit 1
python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
path = Path("decktools/odysseus_start.sh")
content = path.read_text()
marker = "# Launch the application detached from the terminal."
if content.count(marker) != 1:
raise SystemExit("ABORT: Expected exactly one launch section; file unchanged.")
replacement = r'''# Launch the application detached from the terminal.
echo "Starting Odysseus in the background..."
nohup "$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python" -m uvicorn app:app \
--host 0.0.0.0 --port 7011 \
>> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 </dev/null &
APP_PID=$!
echo "$APP_PID" > "$PID_FILE"
# Confirm that a PID belongs to the expected Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null |
grep -Fq "$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app"
}
# Gracefully terminate only the process launched by this script.
cleanup_failed_start() {
echo "Attempting graceful cleanup of failed startup..."
if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
kill -TERM "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
for _ in $(seq 1 10); do
if ! kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
echo "WARNING: Process is still present after 10 seconds."
echo "PID file retained for manual inspection: $PID_FILE"
return 1
fi
else
echo "Process identity no longer matches; no signal sent."
fi
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] && [[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
echo "Failed-start cleanup completed."
}
# Wait up to 30 seconds for the HTTP endpoint.
echo "Waiting for Odysseus HTTP response..."
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
Verify the corrected file
Run this next, still inside the container:
if ! kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus process exited during startup."
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] && [[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
exit 1
fi
HTTP_CODE="$(curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \
--max-time 2 "$APP_HEALTH_URL" 2>/dev/null || true)"
if [[ "$HTTP_CODE" =~ ^[23][0-9][0-9]$ ]]; then
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "ERROR: HTTP responded, but the recorded PID failed identity
verification."
cleanup_failed_start || true
exit 1
fi
echo "Odysseus is responding (HTTP $HTTP_CODE)."
echo "Verified PID: $APP_PID"
echo "Console log: $LOG_FILE"
exit 0
fi
sleep 1
done
echo "ERROR: Odysseus did not respond within 30 seconds."
echo "Inspect: $LOG_FILE"
cleanup_failed_start || true
exit 1
'''
head, separator, _ = content.partition(marker)
path.write_text(head + replacement)
print("Updated startup launch and failure-handling section.")
PY

Step 2.2.3 — Improve process-state verification

/home/deck/OdysseusProject/.venv/bin/python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
path = Path("decktools/odysseus_start.sh")
content = path.read_text()
old = '''# Confirm that a PID belongs to the expected Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
tr '\\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null |
grep -Fq "$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app"
}
'''
new = '''# Confirm that a PID belongs to the expected, non-zombie Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
local state
local cmdline
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/stat" && -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
state="$(awk '{print $3}' "/proc/$pid/stat" 2>/dev/null)" || return 1
[[ "$state" != "Z" && "$state" != "X" ]] || return 1
cmdline="$(tr '\\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null)" || return 1
[[ "$cmdline" == *"$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app"* ]]
}
'''
if content.count(old) != 1:
raise SystemExit("ABORT: Expected process-identity helper not found exactly
once; file unchanged.")
path.write_text(content.replace(old, new))
print("Updated process-state verification.")

41 # Also detect an untracked instance before starting anything.

44 echo "Refusing to start a duplicate. Inspect processes first."

51 --format '{{.State.Running}}')"
52

unchanged."

found."
62 exit 1

>/dev/null; then
69 echo "ChromaDB is healthy."
70 break
71 fi
72 sleep 1
73 done
74

>/dev/null; then

89 # Confirm that a PID belongs to the expected, non-zombie
Odysseus process.
90 is_odysseus_pid() {
91 local pid="$1"
92 local state
93 local cmdline
94
95 [[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
96 [[ -r "/proc/$pid/stat" && -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] ||

97

return 1

100

uvicorn app:app"* ]]

105 # Gracefully terminate only the process launched by this
script.
106 cleanup_failed_start() {
107 echo "Attempting graceful cleanup of failed startup..."
108
109 if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
110 kill -TERM "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
111
112 for _ in $(seq 1 10); do
113 if ! kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
114 break
115 fi
116 sleep 1
117 done
118
119 if kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
120 echo "WARNING: Process is still present after 10
seconds."
121 echo "PID file retained for manual inspection:
$PID_FILE"
122 return 1
123 fi
124 else
125 echo "Process identity no longer matches; no signal
sent."
126 fi
127
128 if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] && [[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" ==
"$APP_PID" ]]; then
129 rm -f "$PID_FILE"
130 fi
131
132 echo "Failed-start cleanup completed."
133 }
134
135 # Wait up to 30 seconds for the HTTP endpoint.
136 echo "Waiting for Odysseus HTTP response..."
137 for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
138 if ! kill -0 "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
139 echo "ERROR: Odysseus process exited during startup."
140 if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] && [[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" ==
"$APP_PID" ]]; then
141 rm -f "$PID_FILE"
142 fi
143 exit 1
144 fi
145
146 HTTP_CODE="$(curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \
ChatGPT said:
147 --max-time 2 "$APP_HEALTH_URL" 2>/dev/null || true)"
148
149 if [[ "$HTTP_CODE" =~ ^[23][0-9][0-9]$ ]]; then
150 if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
151 echo "ERROR: HTTP responded, but the recorded PID
failed identity verification."
152 cleanup_failed_start || true
153 exit 1
154 fi
155
156 echo "Odysseus is responding (HTTP $HTTP_CODE)."
157 echo "Verified PID: $APP_PID"
158 echo "Console log: $LOG_FILE"
159 exit 0
160 fi
161
162 sleep 1
163 done
164
165 echo "ERROR: Odysseus did not respond within 30 seconds."
166 echo "Inspect: $LOG_FILE"
167 cleanup_failed_start || true
168 exit 1

You're right to question this. There are two separate issues here, and I should distinguish them
clearly:
1. The START script did not fail when starting Odysseus. We have not executed it yet.
2. My attempted script correction failed. It was a text-editing operation that expected a
section of the script to appear exactly once. It found a different structure and deliberately
aborted without changing the file.
So this is not evidence that Odysseus, Distrobox, or ChromaDB is malfunctioning. It is an issue
with the way I tried to patch the script. We did not encounter this in the earlier project because
we were not using this particular sequence of guarded text replacements.
And your comparison with Pi-hole is reasonable. Let's look at the architecture itself.
1. Why is ChromaDB in Podman?
The architecture we currently have is:
Steam Deck — SteamOS host
Podman is installed and managed here.
Podman container: odysseus-chromadb
ChromaDB server
Port 127.0.0.1:8100 on the host
Persistent database mounted at /home/deck/OdysseusProject/data/chromadb
Restart policy: no
HTTP connection through the host-published port
Distrobox: odysseus-box
Odysseus application
Python 3.12
Project virtual environment
Uvicorn web server
Agent, tools, memory and RAG
Connects to ChromaDB at 127.0.0.1:8100 from the host-facing network
The important distinction: Odysseus and ChromaDB are two separate processes in two separate
container environments.
2. Why use Podman for ChromaDB?
There are several practical reasons.
This is a reasonable architecture, but it is not the only possible architecture.
Distrobox would have been sufficient to run ChromaDB.
We could have installed ChromaDB's Python package in odysseus-box and started its server
as another process, or used its embedded database functionality where appropriate.
That would give us:
This is simpler from a service-management perspective because both processes live in the
same Distrobox.
The trade-off is that ChromaDB and Odysseus would share the same Python environment and
container lifecycle. Dependency conflicts and upgrades could affect both.
Reason Benefit
Official container image ChromaDB provides a ready-to-run server image
Version pinning We pinned version 1.5.9 and its image digest
Isolation ChromaDB dependencies are separate from Odysseus Python dependencies
Persistent storage Database files survive container replacement
Independent lifecycle ChromaDB can run or stop independently of Odysseus
Easier recovery We can recreate the container without reinstalling the application
Steam Deck
|
└── odysseus-box
|
├── Odysseus / Uvicorn
|
└── ChromaDB server
3. Is ChromaDB more complicated than Pi-hole?
Not inherently.
Your Pi-hole arrangement already demonstrates that you can operate a Podman container with
persistent storage, port mapping and manual startup.
The difference in our current Odysseus setup is where the components live:
The extra complication is the communication and management boundary between Odysseus
and ChromaDB.
For example, the Odysseus START script must:
1. Ask the Steam Deck host's Podman to inspect ChromaDB.
2. Start ChromaDB if it is stopped.
3. Wait until its HTTP health endpoint responds.
4. Start Odysseus.
5. Verify Odysseus responds.
That is additional orchestration, not additional AI complexity.
4. Was the separate container necessary?
No. It was an architectural choice, not a technical requirement.
I would keep the current arrangement for now because:
The ChromaDB container is already installed and working.
Its data directory is persistent.
Its image is pinned.
Its restart policy is no .
Its health endpoint has been verified.
We have already verified that distrobox-host-exec can manage it.
Moving it now would introduce migration work without solving a demonstrated problem.
Component Location Management
Pi-hole pihole-box + Podman container Host Podman
Odysseus odysseus-box Distrobox
ChromaDB Separate Podman container Host Podman
You said:
Worked for 11s
ChatGPT said:
However, I want to make one point clear: we should not let the service scripts become more
complicated than the services they manage.
5. What actually happened with the START script?
There are two separate things:
The START script passed bash -n , meaning its Bash syntax was valid.
My proposed automatic patch failed because one of the expected text sections did not
match exactly.
The patch was trying to move the process-verification function earlier in the file so it could be
used for duplicate detection. The script itself still has its original function placement, and we
have not run it.
I made the correction unnecessarily complicated by trying to modify several sections through
exact text substitutions.
My recommendation: keep Odysseus in Distrobox and ChromaDB in Podman for now. Simplify
the START script rather than redesigning the architecture. We should use the same
straightforward operational principles that worked for Pi-hole: START, STOP, STATUS, CHECK,
and LOGS, with explicit ownership and no automatic boot startup.
The architecture is sound. The script-editing approach is what needs correcting.

cat > decktools/odysseus_start.sh.new <<'BASH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -Eeuo pipefail
PROJECT_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject"
TOOLS_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/decktools"
RUNTIME_DIR="$TOOLS_DIR/runtime"
PID_FILE="$RUNTIME_DIR/odysseus.pid"
CHROMA_MARKER="$RUNTIME_DIR/chromadb.started-by-odysseus"
LOG_FILE="$PROJECT_DIR/data/logs/uvicorn-console.log"
CHROMA_NAME="odysseus-chromadb"
CHROMA_HEALTH_URL="http://127.0.0.1:8100/api/v2/heartbeat"
APP_HEALTH_URL="http://127.0.0.1:7011/"
cd "$PROJECT_DIR"
mkdir -p "$RUNTIME_DIR" "$PROJECT_DIR/data/logs"
host_podman() {
distrobox-host-exec podman "$@"
}
# Verify that a PID is the expected, non-zombie Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
local stat_line
local state
local cmdline
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/stat" && -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
stat_line="$(<"/proc/$pid/stat")" || return 1
state="${stat_line##*) }"
state="${state%% *}"
[[ "$state" != "Z" && "$state" != "X" ]] || return 1
cmdline="$(tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null)" || return 1
[[ "$cmdline" == *"$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app"* ]]
}
echo "===== ODYSSEUS START ====="
# Check the recorded PID first.
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]]; then
OLD_PID="$(cat "$PID_FILE")"
if is_odysseus_pid "$OLD_PID"; then
echo "Odysseus is already running (PID $OLD_PID)."
exit 0
fi
echo "Removing stale or invalid PID file."
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
# Detect an existing instance even if it has no PID file.
for PROC_DIR in /proc/[0-9]*; do
[[ -r "$PROC_DIR/cmdline" ]] || continue
PROC_PID="${PROC_DIR##*/}"
if is_odysseus_pid "$PROC_PID"; then
echo "Existing Odysseus process detected (PID $PROC_PID)."
echo "Refusing to start a duplicate."
exit 1
fi
done
# Check the dedicated ChromaDB container.
if ! host_podman container exists "$CHROMA_NAME"; then
echo "ERROR: Dedicated ChromaDB container was not found."
exit 1
fi
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect "$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" != "true" ]]; then
echo "Starting existing ChromaDB container..."
host_podman start "$CHROMA_NAME" >/dev/null
touch "$CHROMA_MARKER"
else
echo "ChromaDB is already running; ownership remains unchanged."
fi
# Wait up to 30 seconds for ChromaDB.
echo "Waiting for ChromaDB health..."
CHROMA_READY=false
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
if curl -fsS --max-time 2 "$CHROMA_HEALTH_URL" >/dev/null; then
CHROMA_READY=true
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if [[ "$CHROMA_READY" != "true" ]]; then
echo "ERROR: ChromaDB did not become healthy."
echo "If this script started it, the ownership marker is retained."
exit 1
fi
echo "ChromaDB is healthy."
# Launch Odysseus detached from the terminal.
echo "Starting Odysseus in the background..."
nohup "$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python" -m uvicorn app:app \
--host 0.0.0.0 --port 7011 \
>> "$LOG_FILE" 2>&1 </dev/null &
APP_PID=$!
echo "$APP_PID" > "$PID_FILE"
# Gracefully clean up only the process launched by this script.
cleanup_failed_start() {
echo "Attempting graceful cleanup of failed startup..."
if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
kill -TERM "$APP_PID" 2>/dev/null || true
for _ in $(seq 1 10); do
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "WARNING: Process remains active after 10 seconds."
echo "PID file retained for manual inspection: $PID_FILE"
return 1
fi
else
echo "Process identity no longer matches; no signal sent."
fi
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] &&
[[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
echo "Failed-start cleanup completed."
}
# Wait up to 30 seconds for the application.
echo "Waiting for Odysseus HTTP response..."
for _ in $(seq 1 30); do
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus process exited or failed identity verification."
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] &&
[[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
exit 1
fi
HTTP_CODE="$(curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' \
--max-time 2 "$APP_HEALTH_URL" 2>/dev/null || true)"
if [[ "$HTTP_CODE" =~ ^[23][0-9][0-9]$ ]]; then
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "ERROR: HTTP responded, but PID verification failed."
cleanup_failed_start || true
exit 1
fi
echo "Odysseus is responding (HTTP $HTTP_CODE)."
echo "Verified PID: $APP_PID"
echo "Console log: $LOG_FILE"
exit 0
fi
sleep 1
done
echo "ERROR: Odysseus did not respond within 30 seconds."
echo "Inspect: $LOG_FILE"
cleanup_failed_start || true
exit 1
BASH
echo
echo "===== VALIDATE TEMPORARY SCRIPT ====="

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -Eeuo pipefail
PROJECT_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject"
TOOLS_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/decktools"
RUNTIME_DIR="$TOOLS_DIR/runtime"
PID_FILE="$RUNTIME_DIR/odysseus.pid"
CHROMA_MARKER="$RUNTIME_DIR/chromadb.started-by-odysseus"
CHROMA_NAME="odysseus-chromadb"
cd "$PROJECT_DIR"
host_podman() {
distrobox-host-exec podman "$@"
}
# Verify that a PID is the expected, non-zombie Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
local stat_line
local state
local cmdline
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/stat" && -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
stat_line="$(<"/proc/$pid/stat")" || return 1
state="${stat_line##*) }"
state="${state%% *}"
[[ "$state" != "Z" && "$state" != "X" ]] || return 1
cmdline="$(tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null)" || return 1
[[ "$cmdline" == *"$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn app:app"* ]]
}
echo "===== ODYSSEUS STOP ====="
# Stop only the process recorded by the START script.
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]]; then
APP_PID="$(cat "$PID_FILE")"
if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "Requesting graceful shutdown of Odysseus (PID $APP_PID)..."
kill -TERM "$APP_PID"
STOPPED=false
for _ in $(seq 1 20); do
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
STOPPED=true
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if [[ "$STOPPED" != "true" ]]; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus is still running after 20 seconds."
echo "No ChromaDB shutdown will be attempted."
echo "PID file retained: $PID_FILE"
exit 1
fi
echo "Odysseus stopped gracefully."
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] &&
[[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
else
echo "ERROR: PID file exists, but process identity cannot be verified."
echo "No process signal sent and no ChromaDB shutdown attempted."
echo "Inspect PID file: $PID_FILE"
exit 1
fi
else
echo "No Odysseus PID file found."
echo "No application process will be signalled."
fi
# ChromaDB is stopped only when the START script recorded ownership.
if [[ -e "$CHROMA_MARKER" ]]; then
echo "ChromaDB ownership marker found."
if ! host_podman container exists "$CHROMA_NAME"; then
echo "ERROR: Owned ChromaDB container was not found."
echo "Ownership marker retained for inspection."
exit 1
fi
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect "$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" == "true" ]]; then
echo "Stopping ChromaDB started by Odysseus..."
host_podman stop --time 20 "$CHROMA_NAME" >/dev/null
else
echo "ChromaDB is already stopped."
fi
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect "$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" == "true" ]]; then
echo "ERROR: ChromaDB remains running."
echo "Ownership marker retained for inspection."
exit 1
fi
rm -f "$CHROMA_MARKER"
echo "ChromaDB stopped; ownership marker removed."
else
echo "No ChromaDB ownership marker."
You said:
Send me the complete output. We will review the STOP logic before allowing it to stop
anything.
echo "ChromaDB will be left untouched."
fi

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -u
PROJECT_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject"

distrobox-host-exec podman "$@"
}
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
local stat_line
local state

from pathlib import Path

old = '''FOUND_APP=false
FOUND_PID=""
for PROC_DIR in /proc/[0-9]*; do

PROC_PID="${PROC_DIR##*/}"

FOUND_PID=""
FOUND_COUNT=0
for PROC_DIR in /proc/[0-9]*; do
[[ -r "$PROC_DIR/cmdline" ]] || continue
PROC_PID="${PROC_DIR##*/}"
if is_odysseus_pid "$PROC_PID"; then
FOUND_APP=true
FOUND_COUNT=$((FOUND_COUNT + 1))
FOUND_PID="$PROC_PID"
echo "RUNNING: Odysseus process detected (PID $PROC_PID)."
fi
done
if (( FOUND_COUNT > 1 )); then
echo "WARNING: Multiple Odysseus processes detected ($FOUND_COUNT)."
fi
'''

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -uo pipefail
# Steam Deck host dashboard.
# SSH, the pihole container, and the pihole-box Distrobox are
protected.
GREEN=$'\033[32m'
YELLOW=$'\033[33m'
RED=$'\033[31m'
CYAN=$'\033[36m'
RESET=$'\033[0m'
BOLD=$'\033[1m'
LOCALAI_BOX="localai-box"
STABLE_BOX="Stable-box"
PIHOLE_BOX="pihole-box"
PIHOLE_CONTAINER="pihole"
PROJECT="/home/deck/LocalAIProject"
LOCALAI_STOP="$PROJECT/scripts/localai-stop.sh"
SHYRKA_STOP="/home/deck/LocalAIProject/AIAgent/dashboard/w
ebui-stop.sh"
STABLE_STOP="/home/deck/StableDiffusionProject/sd-stop.sh"
say() { printf '%s\n' "$*"; }
good() { printf '%s[OK]%s %s\n' "$GREEN" "$RESET" "$*"; }
warn() { printf '%s[!]%s %s\n' "$YELLOW" "$RESET" "$*"; }
bad() { printf '%s[ERROR]%s %s\n' "$RED" "$RESET" "$*"; }
line() { printf '%s\n' "---------------------------------------------------
---------"; }
ask_yes() {
local answer
printf '%s [y/N] ' "$1"
IFS= read -r answer || answer=""
[[ "$answer" == "y" || "$answer" == "Y" ]]
}
box_state() {
local state
state="$(podman inspect --format '{{.State.Status}}' "$1"
2>/dev/null)" || {
printf 'unknown'
return
}
printf '%s' "$state"
}
box_running() {
[[ "$(box_state "$1")" == "running" ]]
}
# Return codes: 0 = exact process found, 1 = confirmed absent, 2 =
unknown.
# Match complete NUL-separated command lines; never use pgrep
-f here.
process_state() {
local box="$1"
local expected="$2"
local output rc
box_running "$box" || {
local state
state="$(box_state "$box")"
[[ "$state" == "exited" || "$state" == "stopped" ]] && return 1
return 2
}
output="$(podman exec "$box" python3 -c '
import glob, sys
expected = sys.argv[1]
found = False
try:
paths = glob.glob("/proc/[0-9]*/cmdline")
for path in paths:
try:
raw = open(path, "rb").read()
except (OSError, PermissionError):
continue
if not raw:
continue
actual = raw.replace(b"\0", b" ").decode("utf-8",
"replace").rstrip()
if actual == expected:
found = True
break
print("RUNNING" if found else "ABSENT")
except Exception:
print("UNKNOWN")
sys.exit(2)
' "$expected" 2>/dev/null)" || return 2
case "$output" in
RUNNING) return 0 ;;
ABSENT) return 1 ;;
*) return 2 ;;
esac
}
process_running() {
process_state "$1" "$2"
}
# Return codes: 0 = Stable server found, 1 = confirmed absent, 2 =
unknown.
stable_process_state() {
local output
box_running "$STABLE_BOX" || {
local state
state="$(box_state "$STABLE_BOX")"
[[ "$state" == "exited" || "$state" == "stopped" ]] && return 1
return 2
}
output="$(podman exec "$STABLE_BOX" python3 -c '
import glob, os, sys
try:
for path in glob.glob("/proc/[0-9]*/cmdline"):
try:
raw = open(path, "rb").read()
except OSError:
continue
if not raw:
continue
argv = [x.decode("utf-8", "replace") for x in raw.split(b"\0") if x]
if not argv:
continue
if os.path.basename(argv[0]) != "sd-server":
continue
args = argv[1:]
if ("--listen-ip" in args and
args[args.index("--listen-ip") + 1:args.index("--listen-ip") +
2] == ["127.0.0.1"] and
"--listen-port" in args and
args[args.index("--listen-port") + 1:args.index("--listen-port")
+ 2] == ["1234"]):
print("RUNNING")
sys.exit(0)
print("ABSENT")
except Exception:
print("UNKNOWN")
sys.exit(2)
' 2>/dev/null)" || return 2
case "$output" in
RUNNING) return 0 ;;
ABSENT) return 1 ;;
*) return 2 ;;
esac
}
stop_box() {
local box="$1"
case "$box" in
"$PIHOLE_BOX"|"$PIHOLE_CONTAINER")
bad "Refusing to stop protected Pi-hole component: $box"
return 1
;;
esac
if ! box_running "$box"; then
good "$box is already stopped or not running."
return 0
fi
say "Stopping Distrobox gracefully: $box"
if distrobox stop --yes "$box"; then
if ! box_running "$box"; then
good "$box stopped."
return 0
fi
fi
bad "$box did not confirm a stopped state."
return 1
}
run_in_box() {
local box="$1"
local command="$2"
if ! box_running "$box"; then
warn "$box is not running; refusing to enter it for a stop
operation."
return 1
fi
podman exec "$box" bash -lc "$command"
}

shutdown_menu() {
local all=0
local localai_ok=2
local shyrka_ok=2
local stable_ok=2
local answer
local ollama_rc webui_rc shyrka_rc stable_rc
local unknown
local ollama_cmd='/usr/local/bin/ollama serve'
local webui_cmd='/home/deck/LocalAIProject/open-webuivenv/bin/python /home/deck/LocalAIProject/open-webuivenv/bin/open-webui serve --host 192.168.1.161 --port 3000'
local
shyrka_cmd='/home/deck/LocalAIProject/AIAgent/.venv/bin/pytho
n /home/deck/LocalAIProject/AIAgent/dashboard/server.py'
printf '\n%bSHUTDOWN OPTIONS%b\n' "$BOLD" "$RESET"
say "y = ask about eligible services and boxes one by one"
say "a = stop supported services, then eligible boxes"
say "Enter = exit without changes"
printf 'Choose [y/a/Enter]: '
IFS= read -r answer || answer=""
case "$answer" in
a|A) all=1 ;;
y|Y) all=0 ;;
*) say "No changes requested."; return 0 ;;
esac
# Establish LocalAI and Shyrka states independently.
if ! box_running "$LOCALAI_BOX"; then
case "$(box_state "$LOCALAI_BOX")" in
exited|stopped) localai_ok=1; shyrka_ok=1 ;;
*) localai_ok=2; shyrka_ok=2 ;;
esac
else
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$ollama_cmd"; then
ollama_rc=0
else
ollama_rc=$?
fi
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$webui_cmd"; then
webui_rc=0
else
webui_rc=$?
fi
if [[ "$ollama_rc" -eq 2 || "$webui_rc" -eq 2 ]]; then
localai_ok=2
elif [[ "$ollama_rc" -eq 0 || "$webui_rc" -eq 0 ]]; then
localai_ok=0
else
localai_ok=1
fi
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$shyrka_cmd"; then
shyrka_ok=0
else
shyrka_rc=$?
if [[ "$shyrka_rc" -eq 1 ]]; then
shyrka_ok=1
else
shyrka_ok=2
fi
fi
fi
# LocalAI stop + independent post-stop verification.
case "$localai_ok" in
0)
if [[ "$all" -eq 1 ]] || ask_yes "Stop LocalAI (Ollama + Open
WebUI)?"; then
if stop_localai_service; then
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$ollama_cmd"; then
ollama_rc=0
else
ollama_rc=$?
fi
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$webui_cmd"; then
webui_rc=0
else
webui_rc=$?
fi
if [[ "$ollama_rc" -eq 1 && "$webui_rc" -eq 1 ]]; then
localai_ok=1
good "LocalAI processes verified stopped."
elif [[ "$ollama_rc" -eq 2 || "$webui_rc" -eq 2 ]]; then
localai_ok=2
bad "LocalAI state is unknown after the stop script;
its box will be kept running."
else
localai_ok=0
bad "A LocalAI process is still running; its box will be
kept running."
fi
else
localai_ok=0
bad "LocalAI stop script failed; its box will be kept
running."
fi
fi
;;
1) good "LocalAI processes verified absent." ;;
*) warn "LocalAI process state is unknown; refusing its stop
operation and box shutdown." ;;
esac
# Shyrka stop + independent post-stop verification.
case "$shyrka_ok" in
0)
if [[ "$all" -eq 1 ]] || ask_yes "Stop Shyrka Command
Center?"; then
if stop_shyrka_service; then
if process_state "$LOCALAI_BOX" "$shyrka_cmd"; then
shyrka_ok=0
else
shyrka_rc=$?
if [[ "$shyrka_rc" -eq 1 ]]; then
shyrka_ok=1
good "Shyrka process verified stopped."
else
shyrka_ok=2
bad "Shyrka state is unknown after the stop
script."
fi
fi
else
shyrka_ok=0
bad "Shyrka stop script failed; its box will be kept
running."
fi
fi
;;
1) good "Shyrka process verified absent." ;;
*) warn "Shyrka process state is unknown; refusing its stop
operation and box shutdown." ;;
esac
# Stable Diffusion stop + independent post-stop verification.
if ! box_running "$STABLE_BOX"; then
case "$(box_state "$STABLE_BOX")" in
exited|stopped) stable_ok=1 ;;
*) stable_ok=2 ;;
esac
else
if stable_process_state; then
stable_ok=0
else
stable_rc=$?
if [[ "$stable_rc" -eq 1 ]]; then
stable_ok=1
else
stable_ok=2
fi
fi
fi

gracefully?"; then

if stable_process_state; then
stable_ok=0
bad "Stable server is still running; Stable-box will be
kept running."
else
stable_rc=$?
if [[ "$stable_rc" -eq 1 ]]; then
stable_ok=1
good "Stable Diffusion process verified stopped."
else
stable_ok=2
bad "Stable process state is unknown; Stable-box
will be kept running."
fi
fi
else
stable_ok=0
bad "Stable stop script failed; Stable-box will be kept
running."
fi
fi
;;
1) good "Stable Diffusion process verified absent." ;;
*) warn "Stable process state is unknown; refusing its stop
operation and box shutdown." ;;
esac
line
printf '%bBOX SHUTDOWN%b\n' "$BOLD" "$RESET"
if box_running "$LOCALAI_BOX"; then
if [[ "$localai_ok" -eq 1 && "$shyrka_ok" -eq 1 ]]; then
if [[ "$all" -eq 1 ]] || ask_yes "Stop localai-box now?"; then
stop_box "$LOCALAI_BOX" || true
fi
else
warn "Keeping localai-box running: LocalAI and Shyrka must
both be verified stopped."
fi
fi
if box_running "$STABLE_BOX"; then
if [[ "$stable_ok" -eq 1 ]]; then
if [[ "$all" -eq 1 ]] || ask_yes "Stop Stable-box now?"; then
stop_box "$STABLE_BOX" || true
fi
else
warn "Keeping Stable-box running because Stable Diffusion
was not confirmed stopped."
fi
fi
say "Shutdown sequence finished. Review any warnings above."
}

--help|-h)

exit 0
;;
"")

;;

# Return codes: 0 = exact process found, 1 = confirmed absent, 2 = unknown.
process_state() {
local output rc
output="$("$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python" -c '
import glob
import sys
expected = [
"/home/deck/OdysseusProject/.venv/bin/python",
"-m", "uvicorn", "app:app",
"--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "7011"
]
try:
found = []
for path in glob.glob("/proc/[0-9]*/cmdline"):
try:
raw = open(path, "rb").read()
except (OSError, PermissionError):
continue
if not raw:
continue
actual = [
item.decode("utf-8", "replace")
for item in raw.split(b"\0") if item
]
if actual == expected:
pid = path.split("/")[2]
found.append(pid)
if found:
print("RUNNING:" + ",".join(found))
else:
print("ABSENT")
except Exception:
print("UNKNOWN")
sys.exit(2)
' 2>/dev/null)" || return 2
case "$output" in
RUNNING:*) printf '%s\n' "$output"; return 0 ;;
ABSENT) return 1 ;;
*) return 2 ;;
esac
}

a|A) all=1 ;;

fi
RECORDED_PID="$(cat "$PID_FILE")"
if [[ ! "$RECORDED_PID" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]; then
bad "PID file contains an invalid PID."
exit 1
fi
if process_state; then
PROCESS_RESULT="$output"
else
rc=$?

PROCESS_RESULT="ABSENT"

PROCESS_PIDS="${PROCESS_RESULT#RUNNING:}"

bad "Could not send TERM. No ChromaDB shutdown will be attempted."
exit 1
fi
STOPPED=false
for _ in $(seq 1 20); do
if process_state; then
PROCESS_RESULT="$output"
else
rc=$?

STOPPED=true
break
fi
bad "Process state became unknown during shutdown."
exit 1
fi

done
if [[ "$STOPPED" != true ]]; then
bad "Odysseus remains running after 20 seconds."

exit 1

fi

good "No Odysseus ownership marker. ChromaDB will be left untouched."
line

bad "Odysseus is still running. ChromaDB will be left untouched."

bad "Odysseus state is unknown. ChromaDB will be left untouched."

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -Eeuo pipefail
PROJECT_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject"
TOOLS_DIR="$PROJECT_DIR/decktools"
RUNTIME_DIR="$TOOLS_DIR/runtime"
PID_FILE="$RUNTIME_DIR/odysseus.pid"
CHROMA_MARKER="$RUNTIME_DIR/chromadb.started-byodysseus"
CHROMA_NAME="odysseus-chromadb"
cd "$PROJECT_DIR"
host_podman() {
distrobox-host-exec podman "$@"
}
# Verify that a PID is the expected, non-zombie Odysseus process.
is_odysseus_pid() {
local pid="$1"
local stat_line
local state
local cmdline
[[ "$pid" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || return 1
[[ -r "/proc/$pid/stat" && -r "/proc/$pid/cmdline" ]] || return 1
stat_line="$(<"/proc/$pid/stat")" || return 1
state="${stat_line##*) }"
state="${state%% *}"
[[ "$state" != "Z" && "$state" != "X" ]] || return 1
cmdline="$(tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$pid/cmdline" 2>/dev/null)" ||
return 1
[[ "$cmdline" == *"$PROJECT_DIR/.venv/bin/python -m uvicorn
app:app"* ]]
}
echo "===== ODYSSEUS STOP ====="
# Stop only the process recorded by the START script.
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]]; then
APP_PID="$(cat "$PID_FILE")"
if is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
echo "Requesting graceful shutdown of Odysseus (PID
$APP_PID)..."
kill -TERM "$APP_PID"
STOPPED=false
for _ in $(seq 1 20); do
if ! is_odysseus_pid "$APP_PID"; then
STOPPED=true
break
fi
sleep 1
done
if [[ "$STOPPED" != "true" ]]; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus is still running after 20 seconds."
echo "No ChromaDB shutdown will be attempted."
echo "PID file retained: $PID_FILE"
exit 1
fi
echo "Odysseus stopped gracefully."
if [[ -f "$PID_FILE" ]] &&
[[ "$(cat "$PID_FILE")" == "$APP_PID" ]]; then
rm -f "$PID_FILE"
fi
else
echo "ERROR: PID file exists, but process identity cannot be
verified."
echo "No process signal sent and no ChromaDB shutdown
attempted."
echo "Inspect PID file: $PID_FILE"
exit 1
fi
else
echo "No Odysseus PID file found."
echo "No application process will be signalled."
fi
# Never stop ChromaDB while any Odysseus instance is still
running.
for PROC_DIR in /proc/[0-9]*; do
[[ -r "$PROC_DIR/cmdline" ]] || continue
PROC_PID="${PROC_DIR##*/}"
if is_odysseus_pid "$PROC_PID"; then
echo "ERROR: Odysseus is still running (PID $PROC_PID)."
echo "ChromaDB will be left untouched."
exit 1
fi
done
# ChromaDB is stopped only when the START script recorded
ownership.
if [[ -e "$CHROMA_MARKER" ]]; then
echo "ChromaDB ownership marker found."
if ! host_podman container exists "$CHROMA_NAME"; then
echo "ERROR: Owned ChromaDB container was not found."
echo "Ownership marker retained for inspection."
exit 1
fi
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect
"$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" == "true" ]]; then
echo "Stopping ChromaDB started by Odysseus..."
host_podman stop --time 20 "$CHROMA_NAME" >/dev/null
else
echo "ChromaDB is already stopped."
fi
CHROMA_RUNNING="$(host_podman inspect
"$CHROMA_NAME" \
--format '{{.State.Running}}')"
if [[ "$CHROMA_RUNNING" == "true" ]]; then
echo "ERROR: ChromaDB remains running."
echo "Ownership marker retained for inspection."
exit 1
fi
rm -f "$CHROMA_MARKER"
ChatGPT said:
echo "ChromaDB stopped; ownership marker removed."
else
echo "No ChromaDB ownership marker."
echo "ChromaDB will be left untouched."
fi
echo
echo "===== STOP COMPLETE ====="
===== STOP SCRIPT SYNTAX =====
[OK] Bash syntax valid.
(deck@Laco ~)$

We'll use 100 lines per log by default, with an optional argument to request a different
number. The viewer will read the two existing files directly on the host; it will not start Odysseus

1. Create odysseus_logs.sh
STEAM DECK — deck@Laco (HOST)
cat > /home/deck/OdysseusProject/decktools/odysseus_logs.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -Eeuo pipefail
LOG_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusProject/data/logs"
LINES="${1:-100}"
if [[ "$#" -gt 1 || ! "$LINES" =~ ^[1-9][0-9]{0,3}$ ]] || (( LINES > 5000 )); then
printf 'Usage: %s [lines: 1-5000]\n' "$0" >&2
exit 2
fi
show_log() {
local name="$1"
local path="$LOG_DIR/$name"
printf '\n===== %s — last %s lines =====\n' "$name" "$LINES"
if [[ ! -f "$path" ]]; then
printf '[NOT FOUND] %s\n' "$path"
return 0
fi
if [[ ! -r "$path" ]]; then
printf '[NOT READABLE] %s\n' "$path"
return 0
fi
tail -n "$LINES" -- "$path"
}
printf 'ODYSSEUS — READ-ONLY LOG VIEWER\n'
printf 'Log directory: %s\n' "$LOG_DIR"

included.
Send me the syntax, metadata and execution output. We'll verify the utility before marking this
chapter complete.
show_log "app.log"
show_log "uvicorn-console.log"
printf '\n===== END OF LOGS =====\n'
EOF

MANDATORY PROJECT
DOCUMENTATION PROTOCOL
— CURRENT PROJECT
1. SCOPE LOCK — NON-NEGOTIABLE
Document only the project and completed work addressed in the
current conversation.
Establish the target from the current task, verified project path,
files changed, and conversation handover.
Treat the identified project root as the exclusive search
boundary.
Reuse paths and findings already established in this
conversation, but verify the target and relevant files on disk
before writing.
Do not scan unrelated projects, Distroboxes, services, recovery
folders, or the entire filesystem.
If the target project or its canonical path remains ambiguous,
ask one focused clarification question before modifying
anything.
2. TARGETED DOCUMENT DISCOVERY —
MANDATORY
Within the confirmed project scope:
1. Inspect the project root and its established documentation
locations.
2. Locate and verify the authoritative version history, release
notes, and manifest or inventory.
3. Confirm their exact filenames, prefixes, paths, formats, and
relevant existing entries.
4. Exclude backups, archived copies, generated duplicates, and
patches from active editing unless explicitly designated as
authoritative.
5. Reuse the existing documentation set. Do not create
replacement files or introduce a new naming convention
without justification and authorization.
6. If a required file is missing or multiple candidates appear
authoritative, investigate only within the current project and
stop for clarification if unresolved.
Expected documentation roles:
XXX_versions.txt — historical version entries.
XXX_release_notes.txt — historical release descriptions.
XXX_manifest.txt — current inventory, unless its established
format explicitly defines another role.
The XXX prefix and actual spelling must be discovered from the
filesystem, never assumed.
3. EVIDENCE AND VERSION ACCURACY
Record only facts supported by the current conversation, completed
work, or verified project evidence.
Identify what was implemented, what was tested, and what was
independently verified.
Preserve the established versioning scheme. Do not invent
version increments, dates, test results, component versions, or
paths.
Include only changes belonging to the selected project and
release.
If the version or release significance is unclear, resolve it from
existing project conventions or ask before recording it.
Do not describe planned features as implemented.
4. BACKUP BEFORE WRITING
Before changing documentation:
1. Read the current contents and identify the precise changes
required.
2. Create timestamped pre-update copies of every target file in
the project's established documentation-backup location.
3. If no backup location is established, select a safe location
within the current project's designated backup area; if that
cannot be identified, stop and ask.
4. Verify that every backup exists, is readable, and matches its
original file before modification. Never overwrite an existing
backup.
5. Prepare the smallest necessary changes while preserving
encoding, line endings, formatting, and existing history.
Do not modify application code, configuration, dependencies,
databases, services, or unrelated files.
5. UPDATE THE THREE DOCUMENTS
Update the verified documentation set consistently.
Versions: add the completed change using the existing
historical entry format.
Release notes: add a concise, factual description of the change,
relevant fixes, and any verified limitations or migration
requirements.
Manifest: reconcile the current inventory with the actual
resulting project state. Append only if the established manifest
format is historical or append-oriented.
Avoid duplicate entries, unnecessary rewrites, and unrelated
changes.
If any target changes unexpectedly, a write fails, or the actual
project state contradicts the intended documentation, stop and
report the issue.
6. READ-BACK AND INTEGRITY
VERIFICATION — MANDATORY
After writing:
1. Read back every modified document.
2. Confirm the intended entries or inventory changes appear
correctly and only once.
3. Verify that previous history and unrelated content remain
intact.
4. Check file sizes or line counts and calculate SHA-256 hashes
where practical.
5. Verify that all pre-update backups remain readable and match
the original files.
6. Confirm consistency across the three documents.
If verification fails, do not claim success. Restore from a verified preupdate copy only when the failure and safe restoration target are
unambiguous; then verify the restoration and report the outcome.
7. COMPLETION REPORT
Provide a concise audit containing:
Project name and verified canonical path.
Exact paths of the three documentation files.
Version recorded and summary of changes.
Backup paths and backup verification results.
Read-back, consistency, and integrity check results.
Any unresolved issue or skipped check.
Confirmation that unrelated projects and application files were
not modified.
Clearly distinguish completed checks from checks that were not
performed.
8. STOP CONDITION
Finish when the selected documentation has been updated and
verified, or when a blocking issue requires clarification.
Do not expand into cleanup, code changes, installations, service
restarts, or cross-project maintenance.
